He just told me to deal with it. He's always like that, just totally dismissive about everything, especially when the scanners go down. \n\nBut... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my face. Yeah, so whether I'm at the warehouse, or even if I try to go out to salsa night on the weekends, I can just tell everyone is staring at the left side of my face. I'll ask people about it, and they always tell me they don't see anything at all, but I know they're just lying to be polite. I just can't get it out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are staring at your face because they actually see something wrong with it, or because they're trying to mock or hurt you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they're trying to mock me, exactly. I mean, they see it. How could they not see it? It's right there. But I don't think they're, like, plotting against me or anything. It's just human nature to stare at something that's... wrong. \n\nLike, the other day at the warehouse, I caught this new guy looking at me. He just started last week, right after they messed up all our schedules. His name is Greg, I think. Or maybe Craig. He's got this really loud laugh that echoes all the way down aisle 14. Anyway, he was looking right at me while I was trying to fix one of the broken scanners. Those scanners are garbage, by the way. They freeze up if you scan more than three barcodes a minute, and Dave refuses to order new ones because it comes out of his department budget. It's ridiculous. I spend half my shift just rebooting the stupid things. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think Greg—or Craig—was trying to be mean. He was just staring because my face is messed up. I mean, who wouldn't stare?\n\nDoctor: Does your perception of your face being crooked stay the same all the time, or are there moments when it seems less noticeable to you?\n\nPatient: It's... well, it's always there. I never get a break from it. It's just a constant thing, you know? Like, I'll wake up and it's the first thing I think about. \n\nSpeaking of waking up, I've been waking up at 4:30 in the morning now because of that stupid schedule change. 4:30! I used to be able to sleep until 6, which was perfect because then I could grab a coffee at this little diner down the street from my apartment. They make the best hash browns. They're super crispy on the outside but soft in the middle. The lady who works there, Maria, she always knows my order. She's great. But now, at 4:30, they aren't even open yet. So I have to use the vending machine coffee at the warehouse, which tastes like burnt plastic. And it costs two dollars! Two dollars for burnt plastic water. It's a complete rip-off. Dave probably gets a cut of the vending machine profits, honestly. \n\nBut, uh, yeah. It never really seems less noticeable. It's always just... crooked. I can't escape it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face might not be as crooked as you think it is?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think that's possible. I mean, I look in the mirror and it's just obvious. It's right there. I know what I see. \n\nIt's like when you know something is broken, you just know it. Like my car, actually. I drive this 2008 Honda Civic, and last week it started making this weird rattling noise whenever I hit 40 miles an hour. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around in the engine block. I took it to this mechanic near the warehouse—Tony's Auto, I think it's called. Tony looked at it for five minutes and tried to tell me it was just a loose heat shield and wanted to charge me a hundred bucks to tighten a bolt. A hundred bucks! I told him he was crazy. Mechanics are always trying to pull one over on you, you know? They see you coming and just start ringing the cash register in their heads. I ended up just turning the radio up louder so I don't have to hear it. It's mostly sports talk radio anyway, which is fine, except the local guys keep talking about the football team's new quarterback like he's the second coming, and the guy threw four interceptions last Sunday. Four! It's embarrassing. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I know my face is messed up. I'm not imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that other people don't see your face as crooked, even if you're certain that it is?\n\nPatient: I mean, people say they don't see it. Like, the guys at the warehouse, or... or people at salsa night. I go to this salsa class. Well, it used to be on Thursdays, but they moved it to Tuesdays because the instructor, Hector, got a second job bartending and needed Thursdays off to prep or something. Hector's alright, but he wears these intensely shiny, pointy shoes that just squeak on the hardwood floor the entire time. Squeak, squeak, squeak, for a whole hour. And the floor at the community center is totally warped over by the mirrors anyway. You step on this one spot and the whole board just dips down like a sponge. Somebody is definitely going to twist an ankle on that thing. I actually almost tripped on it last week when I was doing a turn with this girl, Sarah. \n\nBut, um... yeah, Sarah, or Hector, or anyone else there, they'll tell me they don't see anything wrong with me. But I just can't stop thinking about it. I know the left side of my face looks crooked and completely messed up. Even when they tell me they don't see anything, I know they're noticing it. I can just feel them looking at it.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the left side of your face being crooked affected your ability to do your job at the warehouse or enjoy activities like salsa night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. I actually got written up at the warehouse recently because I keep falling behind on my orders. I just... I get so distracted thinking about my face, or I have to keep going to the bathroom to check it in the mirror. \n\nSpeaking of the bathroom at work, it's a disaster. The soap dispenser has been broken for three weeks. Three weeks! You push the button and it just oozes this weird, watery blue liquid that smells like cheap pine cleaner. It doesn't even lather. I told Dave about it, and he just mumbled something about putting in a work order. A work order for a soap dispenser? Just go to the store and buy a new one! It's not rocket science. And the paper towel dispenser is one of those motion-sensor ones, but you have to wave your hand like a maniac for a solid minute just to get one tiny square of paper. It drives me crazy. \n\nBut, uh, yeah, my work is definitely suffering. And I've actually stopped going to salsa night altogether. I skip my Sunday soccer games now, too. Even family gatherings... I just can't do it. I don't want to be around people because I know they're staring at my crooked face, and God forbid someone tries to take a picture. My aunt Susan is always taking pictures at family stuff with this massive iPad. Who uses an iPad as a camera? It's the size of a dinner plate. She holds it up with both hands and blocks everyone's view. It's ridiculous. But yeah, I just avoid all of it now. It's easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face is actually fine and you're just perceiving it as crooked?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't see how that could be it. How could my brain just make up something that specific? It's right there. I see it every single time I look in a mirror. Like the mirrors over at Sal's Barbershop. He's got those huge wrap-around ones, right? \n\nActually, Sal just raised his prices again. Jumped from fifteen to twenty-two dollars for a basic fade. He blames it on inflation, but I know for a fact he just bought a new boat. He showed me pictures of it on his phone while I was in the chair. It's this ridiculous fiberglass speedboat with neon green racing stripes. Who puts neon green on a boat? And he was complaining the whole time about the docking fees down at the marina, like I'm supposed to feel sorry for him while I'm paying twenty-two bucks for a haircut that takes him ten minutes. He doesn't even use the hot towel on your neck anymore! He claims the towel warmer shorted out, but it's been sitting in the corner unplugged since October. I think he's just trying to cut corners to pay for the marina fees. \n\nBut... no, I'm not just perceiving it wrong. It's real. I'm not crazy, I know what I'm looking at. My face is definitely crooked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that a doctor or a plastic surgeon might not see anything wrong with the left side of your face if they examined it?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe they wouldn't see it either, I guess. Or they'd pretend not to. But honestly, that's why I'm here, because I just can't stop thinking about it. I'm totally preoccupied with it. I just know the left side of my face looks crooked and messed up, and it feels like everyone at the warehouse or at salsa night is noticing it, even when they tell me they don't see anything. \n\nLike this one plastic surgeon I looked up online, Dr. Halloway. His office is downtown, right across from that new coffee shop, The Roasted Bean. Have you been there? They charge seven dollars for a latte. Seven dollars! And they don't even have regular milk, it's all oat milk or macadamia nut milk or whatever. I asked for just regular whole milk and the barista looked at me like I'd asked for motor oil. She had this tiny little beanie perched on the very back of her head, barely holding on. I don't understand how it doesn't just slide off when she leans over the espresso machine. And the tables in there are made of these raw, reclaimed wood slabs, which look cool, but they're so uneven you can't even set your seven-dollar coffee down without it wobbling and spilling everywhere. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if a surgeon would see it or not. But I know it's there. I can't get it out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your concern about your face is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I don't... no. No, it's definitely real. I mean, I see it. It's not like I'm hallucinating or making it up. It's right there on my face. How could it not be based in reality when I can point right to it? \n\nSpeaking of reality, my buddy Mark from the warehouse—he's the guy who drives the forklift on the second shift—he was just telling me about this virtual reality headset he bought. He dropped like six hundred bucks on it. Six hundred dollars! And he lives in this tiny studio apartment above a dry cleaner. You can smell the chemicals through the floorboards. But he's got this massive VR setup right in the middle of his living room. He says he uses it to play ping pong. Why would you spend six hundred dollars to play fake ping pong when you could just go to the rec center and play real ping pong for free? And he knocked over a lamp while he was doing it, shattered the bulb everywhere, and he didn't even realize it until he took the headset off an hour later. \n\nBut no, I'm not in some virtual reality. My face is crooked. It's real, and it's ruining my life. I wouldn't be missing salsa night if it wasn't real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're mistakenly convinced it's crooked?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess other people say it looks normal. Like, they tell me they don't see it. But they're just... they're just not looking close enough, or they're trying to be nice. It's not a mistake, I know what I see. \n\nMy aunt Linda is like that, always trying to be nice instead of just telling the truth. She brought over this casserole last Thanksgiving—sweet potato with marshmallows on top, right? But she accidentally used salt instead of sugar in the sweet potato mix. It was awful. Just incredibly salty. But my uncle Jerry, he just sat there eating it, smiling and saying how great it was, sweating through his shirt because it was so salty. He drank like four glasses of water right at the table. Why wouldn't he just tell her? Now she thinks we all love it, and she made it again for Easter. I had to pretend I was on some strict low-carb diet just to get out of eating it. \n\nBut with my face... it's not like the casserole. It's not a mistake on my end. I'm not convinced of something that isn't true. They just don't want to admit how bad it really looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still believe your face is crooked even if multiple doctors told you there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know. I haven't asked a bunch of doctors. If like, three of them lined up and said it was fine? I guess... I'd probably just think they were missing it. But honestly, getting multiple doctors together sounds like a nightmare. Have you ever tried to even get one appointment lately? \n\nI had to go to urgent care a couple months ago because this new guy at the warehouse, Kyle, backed a manual pallet jack right into my ankle. Kyle's an absolute idiot, man. He wears his high-vis vest completely unzipped and listens to podcasts on his AirPods while he's pulling heavy machinery. Management doesn't even say anything to him because his uncle is the shift supervisor. \n\nAnyway, I go to the clinic, and I'm sitting in the waiting room for three hours. Three hours! And they had this huge fish tank built right into the wall, but it only had one sad little goldfish in it. Just swimming in circles. The water was all cloudy, like nobody had cleaned the filter in a year, and there was this weird green algae growing on the little plastic treasure chest at the bottom. I almost asked the receptionist if I could just take the fish home because it was so depressing to watch. And the magazines on the table were from like, 2018. Who wants to read a five-year-old copy of Better Homes and Gardens while their ankle is throbbing? \n\nSo yeah, I don't really know what I'd think if a bunch of doctors told me my face was fine. I'm not sure. I just... I know what I see in the mirror every morning, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being crooked would change if you saw a video or photo of yourself where your face looked normal?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't know. I guess I haven't really seen a picture like that. Every time someone takes a photo of me, I can see it. It's so obvious. But maybe if there was some magic video where it looked normal? I don't know, I'd probably just think it was photoshopped or a weird angle or something. People do that all the time now with those filters. \n\nMy cousin Sarah, she just got married last month. She hired this really expensive photographer, right? And when she got the pictures back, she was furious because the guy had edited out her husband's mole. Like, he has this big mole right on his cheek, and the photographer just airbrushed it away without even asking. Her husband was actually kind of offended, but Sarah was mad because she said it looked like she married a mannequin. They paid like two thousand dollars for these pictures, and they're fighting with the guy to get the unedited ones back. And the catering at the wedding was terrible, too. They had this chicken that was so dry you could barely swallow it, and they ran out of the little crab cakes during cocktail hour before I even got one. I was looking forward to those crab cakes all week. \n\nBut yeah, if I saw a picture where my face looked normal... I just don't think I'd believe it. I mean, the mirror doesn't lie, right? I know what's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would still appear crooked to you even if a plastic surgeon or a doctor examined it and found nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know. I've never actually been to a plastic surgeon. If they told me nothing was wrong... I'd probably just think they were blind, or maybe they just didn't want to deal with me. Honestly, I don't really trust plastic surgeons anyway. My buddy Dave, he works the loading dock with me, his girlfriend went to one of those places last month to get her lips done. You know, the fillers? She came back looking like she got stung right in the mouth by a whole nest of hornets. It was ridiculous. Dave was complaining about it for a solid week while we were running the cardboard baler. \n\nWhich, by the way, that baler has been jamming constantly. Management refuses to call the maintenance company to fix the hydraulic press, so every time we crush a load of boxes, the heavy metal plate gets stuck halfway up. We literally have to pry it loose with a crowbar just to keep working. It's super dangerous, but they don't care as long as the trucks get loaded on time. \n\nBut yeah, Dave's girlfriend spent like eight hundred bucks just to look like a cartoon character, and the doctor told her it looked great. So if a plastic surgeon told me my face was perfectly straight? I don't know what I'd think. I just wouldn't buy it. I look in the mirror and the crookedness is right there, no matter what anyone else says.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean... a video from all angles? I don't know. I've never seen a video like that where it actually looks normal. I'd probably just think the camera lens was warped or something. 

Speaking of cameras from different angles, they actually just installed all these new 360-degree dome cameras on the ceiling at the warehouse. Management sent out this whole memo saying it's for inventory control, but we all know they're just spying on us to make sure we aren't taking extra bathroom breaks. My buddy Dave got written up on Tuesday because the camera caught him sitting on a pallet of bulk dog food for like, two minutes. Two minutes! And the crazy thing is, the footage is so cheap and grainy, you can't even really tell it's him. It just looks like a blurry blob in a neon vest. 

The security guard, Gary, he sits in this little booth by the loading dock all day eating those powdered mini donuts right out of the bag, and he gets white powder all over the keyboards and the monitors. It's completely disgusting. You can barely see the screens through the donut dust anyway. 

So honestly, cameras distort everything. If someone showed me a video where my face was perfectly straight... I just wouldn't trust the video. I wouldn't believe it. I know what my face looks like, you know? It's completely crooked.

A false belief? Like... like I'm just imagining it? No way. I'm not crazy. I know exactly what I see when I look in the mirror. It's not some fake reality, it's right there on my face. 

It's actually a lot like that new inventory software they just forced us to start using at the warehouse. The corporate guys swear by it, right? They literally called it the "new reality" of our supply chain in this dumb training video they made us watch. But the hand scanners—these little Zebra devices we have to carry around—they constantly glitch out. Last Thursday, the system said we had twelve pallets of the blue frost Gatorade in aisle four. Our new supervisor, Craig, he just transferred from the regional office and wears these shiny dress shoes on the concrete floor, which is ridiculous. Anyway, he comes down and starts yelling at us to load the Gatorade. We tell him, Craig, the racks are empty. There is no Gatorade. But he just keeps tapping his tablet, saying, "The system says it's here, so it's here." 

We literally spent three hours walking up and down a freezing cold warehouse looking for phantom sports drinks just because his screen told him it was reality. And don't even get me started on the forklift tires. They're completely bald, but Craig won't sign off on new ones because his computer says they have another six months of tread left. Dave almost slid right into a support beam yesterday because there's absolutely no grip left on the rubber. 

But yeah, no. I'm not like Craig staring at a broken screen and believing something that isn't there. I'm looking at my actual face. I'm not making it up. It's crooked, and that's just the reality of it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, a medical report? From a whole team of doctors? I don't know. Like I said, I’m here because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks crooked and messed up. It's just... it's all I think about. Like everyone at the warehouse or at salsa night is noticing it, even when they tell me they don’t see anything. So a piece of paper isn't going to change what I see in the mirror. 

Speaking of salsa night, though, they completely changed the layout at the club downtown where I go on Thursdays. They moved the DJ booth all the way to the back near the emergency exit, which makes zero sense because now the bass just bounces off the concrete wall and sounds completely muffled. And the guy who runs it—Hector—he decided to put these new strobe lights right above the main dance floor. It's awful. You're trying to do a basic cross-body lead, and suddenly you're blinded by this flashing purple light. My buddy Dave came with me last week, and he actually tripped over someone's jacket that got left on the floor because he couldn't see anything. 

Dave was so mad, he went to get a drink and the bartender tried to charge him fourteen dollars for a well rum and coke. Fourteen dollars! And it was in one of those tiny plastic cups they use for water coolers. Dave told the guy to keep it and we ended up just leaving early and getting tacos from the truck parked outside the muffler shop on 5th Street. Best al pastor in the city, honestly. The guy who runs the truck, he marinates the pork for like two days straight. 

But yeah, back to the doctors... I just wouldn't believe them. I know they went to medical school and everything, but they aren't the ones walking around with my face. I am. And it's crooked.

Mechanics are always trying to pull one over on you, you know? They see you coming and just start ringing the cash register in their heads. I ended up just turning the radio up louder so I don't have to hear it. Well, it used to be on Thursdays, but they moved it to Tuesdays because the instructor, Hector, got a second job bartending and needed Thursdays off to prep or something. She holds it up with both hands and blocks everyone's view. It's ridiculous. But yeah, I just avoid all of it now. He drank like four glasses of water right at the table. Why wouldn't he just tell her? Now she thinks we all love it, and she made it again for Easter. A video taken by someone I trust? Like Dave? I mean... I guess I'd trust Dave not to mess with the camera, but I'd still think my face was crooked. It wouldn't matter who was holding the camera. 

Actually, Dave tried to take a video of me at the warehouse a couple of weeks ago. He kept bragging about the megapixel count and the "cinematic mode" or whatever. We were on break, sitting on some empty pallets near the loading bay, and he just starts filming me eating my sandwich. I hate it when people do that. I was eating a turkey on rye from that deli down the street—the one where the guy always gives you way too much mayo unless you specifically tell him not to. I swear, last time I went there, the bread was completely soggy before I even got back to work. 

Anyway, Dave is pointing this stove-top phone at me, and the flash is on for some reason even though it's the middle of the day, and I just covered my face. I told him to delete it right then and there. I couldn't even look at the screen to see if he deleted it because I knew I'd just see how lopsided my jaw is. 

He was pretty annoyed, actually. He spent the rest of the break playing some racing game on his phone with the volume turned all the way up. The sound effects were so loud, Gary the security guard actually came over and told him to turn it down. Gary was eating his powdered donuts, of course, and he got a smudge of white powder right on Dave's new screen when he pointed at it. Dave was furious. He spent like ten minutes trying to wipe it off with his neon vest, but those vests are made of that cheap scratchy material, so it just smeared the donut grease around. 

But yeah, even if Dave took a video of me from every angle and swore up and down that I looked normal, I wouldn't believe it. The camera doesn't fix my face, you know? It's just... it's crooked. I can feel it. I know it is.

A family member? Like my sister Sarah? I mean... no, honestly. Look, I know I'm repeating myself here, but like I said earlier, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this chair is because I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's constantly in my head. I feel like every single person at the warehouse is staring at it, or the people at salsa night, even when they swear to me they don't see anything wrong. So Sarah showing me a video isn't going to magically make me believe them. 

She actually did have her camera out at my nephew's birthday party a couple weeks ago. He just turned seven. They rented one of those giant inflatable obstacle courses for the backyard, but the guy who delivered it forgot the tarp that goes underneath to protect the grass. So my brother-in-law Mark had to drive over to the hardware store on a Saturday morning to buy one. You ever try going to the hardware store on a weekend? It's absolute chaos. People just leave those heavy-duty flatbed carts right in the middle of the aisles, and the contractors act like they own the place. Mark was gone for like two hours, and by the time he got back, the kids had already gotten into the coolers and drank all the sodas. They were just running around the yard completely wired on sugar, chasing the dog. 

And Mark ended up buying this cheap, thin plastic tarp that ripped the second they dragged the inflatable over it anyway. He was sweating through his shirt trying to tape it together with duct tape, getting so mad at the delivery guy who was just sitting in his truck smoking a cigarette. 

So Sarah is standing there filming this whole disaster, and she points her phone at me while I'm holding a paper plate with a hot dog on it. I just turned around and walked into the house. I don't care if she's my sister, I know what I look like. A video from her is just going to show exactly what I already see in the mirror.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, hi. Yeah, thanks. I guess I'm here because... well, I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked. It's just messed up, you know? And I know everyone is noticing it. \n\nLike at the warehouse where I work—I do order filling, stocking, that kind of stuff. It's a huge facility. Actually, they just changed our shift schedules last week without even asking us, which really threw off my whole routine, and my supervisor Dave didn't even care when I brought it up. He just told me to deal with it. He's always like that, just totally dismissive about everything, especially when the scanners go down. \n\nBut... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my face. Yeah, so whether I'm at the warehouse, or even if I try to go out to salsa night on the weekends, I can just tell everyone is staring at the left side of my face. I'll ask people about it, and they always tell me they don't see anything at all, but I know they're just lying to be polite. I just can't get it out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are staring at your face because they actually see something wrong with it, or because they're trying to mock or hurt you in some way?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they're trying to mock me, exactly. I mean, they see it. How could they not see it? It's right there. But I don't think they're, like, plotting against me or anything. It's just human nature to stare at something that's... wrong. \n\nLike, the other day at the warehouse, I caught this new guy looking at me. He just started last week, right after they messed up all our schedules. His name is Greg, I think. Or maybe Craig. He's got this really loud laugh that echoes all the way down aisle 14. Anyway, he was looking right at me while I was trying to fix one of the broken scanners. Those scanners are garbage, by the way. They freeze up if you scan more than three barcodes a minute, and Dave refuses to order new ones because it comes out of his department budget. It's ridiculous. I spend half my shift just rebooting the stupid things. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think Greg—or Craig—was trying to be mean. He was just staring because my face is messed up. I mean, who wouldn't stare?\n\nDoctor: Does your perception of your face being crooked stay the same all the time, or are there moments when it seems less noticeable to you?\n\nPatient: It's... well, it's always there. I never get a break from it. It's just a constant thing, you know? Like, I'll wake up and it's the first thing I think about. \n\nSpeaking of waking up, I've been waking up at 4:30 in the morning now because of that stupid schedule change. 4:30! I used to be able to sleep until 6, which was perfect because then I could grab a coffee at this little diner down the street from my apartment. They make the best hash browns. They're super crispy on the outside but soft in the middle. The lady who works there, Maria, she always knows my order. She's great. But now, at 4:30, they aren't even open yet. So I have to use the vending machine coffee at the warehouse, which tastes like burnt plastic. And it costs two dollars! Two dollars for burnt plastic water. It's a complete rip-off. Dave probably gets a cut of the vending machine profits, honestly. \n\nBut, uh, yeah. It never really seems less noticeable. It's always just... crooked. I can't escape it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face might not be as crooked as you think it is?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't think that's possible. I mean, I look in the mirror and it's just obvious. It's right there. I know what I see. \n\nIt's like when you know something is broken, you just know it. Like my car, actually. I drive this 2008 Honda Civic, and last week it started making this weird rattling noise whenever I hit 40 miles an hour. It sounds like there's a bunch of loose change rattling around in the engine block. I took it to this mechanic near the warehouse—Tony's Auto, I think it's called. Tony looked at it for five minutes and tried to tell me it was just a loose heat shield and wanted to charge me a hundred bucks to tighten a bolt. A hundred bucks! I told him he was crazy. Mechanics are always trying to pull one over on you, you know? They see you coming and just start ringing the cash register in their heads. I ended up just turning the radio up louder so I don't have to hear it. It's mostly sports talk radio anyway, which is fine, except the local guys keep talking about the football team's new quarterback like he's the second coming, and the guy threw four interceptions last Sunday. Four! It's embarrassing. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I know my face is messed up. I'm not imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that other people don't see your face as crooked, even if you're certain that it is?\n\nPatient: I mean, people say they don't see it. Like, the guys at the warehouse, or... or people at salsa night. I go to this salsa class. Well, it used to be on Thursdays, but they moved it to Tuesdays because the instructor, Hector, got a second job bartending and needed Thursdays off to prep or something. Hector's alright, but he wears these intensely shiny, pointy shoes that just squeak on the hardwood floor the entire time. Squeak, squeak, squeak, for a whole hour. And the floor at the community center is totally warped over by the mirrors anyway. You step on this one spot and the whole board just dips down like a sponge. Somebody is definitely going to twist an ankle on that thing. I actually almost tripped on it last week when I was doing a turn with this girl, Sarah. \n\nBut, um... yeah, Sarah, or Hector, or anyone else there, they'll tell me they don't see anything wrong with me. But I just can't stop thinking about it. I know the left side of my face looks crooked and completely messed up. Even when they tell me they don't see anything, I know they're noticing it. I can just feel them looking at it.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the left side of your face being crooked affected your ability to do your job at the warehouse or enjoy activities like salsa night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. I actually got written up at the warehouse recently because I keep falling behind on my orders. I just... I get so distracted thinking about my face, or I have to keep going to the bathroom to check it in the mirror. \n\nSpeaking of the bathroom at work, it's a disaster. The soap dispenser has been broken for three weeks. Three weeks! You push the button and it just oozes this weird, watery blue liquid that smells like cheap pine cleaner. It doesn't even lather. I told Dave about it, and he just mumbled something about putting in a work order. A work order for a soap dispenser? Just go to the store and buy a new one! It's not rocket science. And the paper towel dispenser is one of those motion-sensor ones, but you have to wave your hand like a maniac for a solid minute just to get one tiny square of paper. It's easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face is actually fine and you're just perceiving it as crooked?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't see how that could be it. But I know it's there. I can't get it out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your concern about your face is not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I don't... no. No, it's definitely real. I mean, I see it. It's not like I'm hallucinating or making it up. It's right there on my face. How could it not be based in reality when I can point right to it? \n\nSpeaking of reality, my buddy Mark from the warehouse—he's the guy who drives the forklift on the second shift—he was just telling me about this virtual reality headset he bought. He dropped like six hundred bucks on it. Six hundred dollars! And he lives in this tiny studio apartment above a dry cleaner. You can smell the chemicals through the floorboards. But he's got this massive VR setup right in the middle of his living room. He says he uses it to play ping pong. Why would you spend six hundred dollars to play fake ping pong when you could just go to the rec center and play real ping pong for free? And he knocked over a lamp while he was doing it, shattered the bulb everywhere, and he didn't even realize it until he took the headset off an hour later. \n\nBut no, I'm not in some virtual reality. My face is crooked. It's real, and it's ruining my life. I wouldn't be missing salsa night if it wasn't real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face appears normal to others, but you're mistakenly convinced it's crooked?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess other people say it looks normal. Like, they tell me they don't see it. But they're just... they're just not looking close enough, or they're trying to be nice. It's not a mistake, I know what I see. \n\nMy aunt Linda is like that, always trying to be nice instead of just telling the truth. She brought over this casserole last Thanksgiving—sweet potato with marshmallows on top, right? But she accidentally used salt instead of sugar in the sweet potato mix. It was awful. Just incredibly salty. But my uncle Jerry, he just sat there eating it, smiling and saying how great it was, sweating through his shirt because it was so salty. He drank like four glasses of water right at the table. Why wouldn't he just tell her? Now she thinks we all love it, and she made it again for Easter. I had to pretend I was on some strict low-carb diet just to get out of eating it. \n\nBut with my face... it's not like the casserole. It's not a mistake on my end. I'm not convinced of something that isn't true. They just don't want to admit how bad it really looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still believe your face is crooked even if multiple doctors told you there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know. I haven't asked a bunch of doctors. If like, three of them lined up and said it was fine? I guess... I'd probably just think they were missing it. But honestly, getting multiple doctors together sounds like a nightmare. Have you ever tried to even get one appointment lately? \n\nI had to go to urgent care a couple months ago because this new guy at the warehouse, Kyle, backed a manual pallet jack right into my ankle. Kyle's an absolute idiot, man. He wears his high-vis vest completely unzipped and listens to podcasts on his AirPods while he's pulling heavy machinery. Management doesn't even say anything to him because his uncle is the shift supervisor. \n\nAnyway, I go to the clinic, and I'm sitting in the waiting room for three hours. Three hours! And they had this huge fish tank built right into the wall, but it only had one sad little goldfish in it. Just swimming in circles. The water was all cloudy, like nobody had cleaned the filter in a year, and there was this weird green algae growing on the little plastic treasure chest at the bottom. I almost asked the receptionist if I could just take the fish home because it was so depressing to watch. And the magazines on the table were from like, 2018. Who wants to read a five-year-old copy of Better Homes and Gardens while their ankle is throbbing? \n\nSo yeah, I don't really know what I'd think if a bunch of doctors told me my face was fine. I'm not sure. I just... I know what I see in the mirror every morning, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being crooked would change if you saw a video or photo of yourself where your face looked normal?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't know. I guess I haven't really seen a picture like that. Every time someone takes a photo of me, I can see it. It's so obvious. But maybe if there was some magic video where it looked normal? I don't know, I'd probably just think it was photoshopped or a weird angle or something. People do that all the time now with those filters. \n\nMy cousin Sarah, she just got married last month. She hired this really expensive photographer, right? And when she got the pictures back, she was furious because the guy had edited out her husband's mole. Like, he has this big mole right on his cheek, and the photographer just airbrushed it away without even asking. Her husband was actually kind of offended, but Sarah was mad because she said it looked like she married a mannequin. They paid like two thousand dollars for these pictures, and they're fighting with the guy to get the unedited ones back. And the catering at the wedding was terrible, too. They had this chicken that was so dry you could barely swallow it, and they ran out of the little crab cakes during cocktail hour before I even got one. I was looking forward to those crab cakes all week. \n\nBut yeah, if I saw a picture where my face looked normal... I just don't think I'd believe it. I mean, the mirror doesn't lie, right? I know what's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would still appear crooked to you even if a plastic surgeon or a doctor examined it and found nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know. I've never actually been to a plastic surgeon. If they told me nothing was wrong... I'd probably just think they were blind, or maybe they just didn't want to deal with me. Honestly, I don't really trust plastic surgeons anyway. My buddy Dave, he works the loading dock with me, his girlfriend went to one of those places last month to get her lips done. You know, the fillers? She came back looking like she got stung right in the mouth by a whole nest of hornets. It was ridiculous. Dave was complaining about it for a solid week while we were running the cardboard baler. \n\nWhich, by the way, that baler has been jamming constantly. Management refuses to call the maintenance company to fix the hydraulic press, so every time we crush a load of boxes, the heavy metal plate gets stuck halfway up. We literally have to pry it loose with a crowbar just to keep working. It's super dangerous, but they don't care as long as the trucks get loaded on time. \n\nBut yeah, Dave's girlfriend spent like eight hundred bucks just to look like a cartoon character, and the doctor told her it looked great. So if a plastic surgeon told me my face was perfectly straight? I don't know what I'd think. I just wouldn't buy it. I look in the mirror and the crookedness is right there, no matter what anyone else says.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would still appear crooked to you even if you saw a video of yourself from multiple angles where your face looked perfectly normal?\n\nPatient: I mean... a video from all angles? I don't know. I've never seen a video like that where it actually looks normal. I'd probably just think the camera lens was warped or something. \n\nSpeaking of cameras from different angles, they actually just installed all these new 360-degree dome cameras on the ceiling at the warehouse. Management sent out this whole memo saying it's for inventory control, but we all know they're just spying on us to make sure we aren't taking extra bathroom breaks. My buddy Dave got written up on Tuesday because the camera caught him sitting on a pallet of bulk dog food for like, two minutes. Two minutes! And the crazy thing is, the footage is so cheap and grainy, you can't even really tell it's him. It just looks like a blurry blob in a neon vest. \n\nThe security guard, Gary, he sits in this little booth by the loading dock all day eating those powdered mini donuts right out of the bag, and he gets white powder all over the keyboards and the monitors. It's completely disgusting. You can barely see the screens through the donut dust anyway. \n\nSo honestly, cameras distort everything. If someone showed me a video where my face was perfectly straight... I just wouldn't trust the video. I wouldn't believe it. I know what my face looks like, you know? It's completely crooked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your belief about your face being crooked is a false belief that's not based on reality?\n\nPatient: A false belief? Like... like I'm just imagining it? No way. I'm not crazy. I know exactly what I see when I look in the mirror. It's not some fake reality, it's right there on my face. \n\nIt's actually a lot like that new inventory software they just forced us to start using at the warehouse. The corporate guys swear by it, right? They literally called it the \"new reality\" of our supply chain in this dumb training video they made us watch. But the hand scanners—these little Zebra devices we have to carry around—they constantly glitch out. Last Thursday, the system said we had twelve pallets of the blue frost Gatorade in aisle four. Our new supervisor, Craig, he just transferred from the regional office and wears these shiny dress shoes on the concrete floor, which is ridiculous. Anyway, he comes down and starts yelling at us to load the Gatorade. We tell him, Craig, the racks are empty. There is no Gatorade. But he just keeps tapping his tablet, saying, \"The system says it's here, so it's here.\" \n\nWe literally spent three hours walking up and down a freezing cold warehouse looking for phantom sports drinks just because his screen told him it was reality. And don't even get me started on the forklift tires. They're completely bald, but Craig won't sign off on new ones because his computer says they have another six months of tread left. Dave almost slid right into a support beam yesterday because there's absolutely no grip left on the rubber. \n\nBut yeah, no. I'm not like Craig staring at a broken screen and believing something that isn't there. I'm looking at my actual face. I'm not making it up. It's crooked, and that's just the reality of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about your face being crooked would change if you were shown a medical report from a team of doctors confirming that there is nothing physically wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: I mean, a medical report? From a whole team of doctors? I don't know. Like I said, I’m here because I can’t stop thinking that the left side of my face looks crooked and messed up. It's just... it's all I think about. Like everyone at the warehouse or at salsa night is noticing it, even when they tell me they don’t see anything. So a piece of paper isn't going to change what I see in the mirror. \n\nSpeaking of salsa night, though, they completely changed the layout at the club downtown where I go on Thursdays. They moved the DJ booth all the way to the back near the emergency exit, which makes zero sense because now the bass just bounces off the concrete wall and sounds completely muffled. And the guy who runs it—Hector—he decided to put these new strobe lights right above the main dance floor. It's awful. You're trying to do a basic cross-body lead, and suddenly you're blinded by this flashing purple light. My buddy Dave came with me last week, and he actually tripped over someone's jacket that got left on the floor because he couldn't see anything. \n\nDave was so mad, he went to get a drink and the bartender tried to charge him fourteen dollars for a well rum and coke. Fourteen dollars! And it was in one of those tiny plastic cups they use for water coolers. Dave told the guy to keep it and we ended up just leaving early and getting tacos from the truck parked outside the muffler shop on 5th Street. Best al pastor in the city, honestly. The guy who runs the truck, he marinates the pork for like two days straight. \n\nBut yeah, back to the doctors... I just wouldn't believe them. I know they went to medical school and everything, but they aren't the ones walking around with my face. I am. And it's crooked.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would still appear crooked to you even if you were shown a video of yourself from multiple angles, taken by someone you trust, where your face looks perfectly normal?\n\nPatient: A video taken by someone I trust? Like Dave? I mean... I guess I'd trust Dave not to mess with the camera, but I'd still think my face was crooked. It wouldn't matter who was holding the camera. \n\nActually, Dave tried to take a video of me at the warehouse a couple of weeks ago. He got this new phone, one of those ridiculously expensive ones with the three lenses on the back that look like a stove top. He was so proud of it. He kept bragging about the megapixel count and the \"cinematic mode\" or whatever. We were on break, sitting on some empty pallets near the loading bay, and he just starts filming me eating my sandwich. I hate it when people do that. I was eating a turkey on rye from that deli down the street—the one where the guy always gives you way too much mayo unless you specifically tell him not to. I swear, last time I went there, the bread was completely soggy before I even got back to work. \n\nAnyway, Dave is pointing this stove-top phone at me, and the flash is on for some reason even though it's the middle of the day, and I just covered my face. I told him to delete it right then and there. I couldn't even look at the screen to see if he deleted it because I knew I'd just see how lopsided my jaw is. \n\nHe was pretty annoyed, actually. He spent the rest of the break playing some racing game on his phone with the volume turned all the way up. The sound effects were so loud, Gary the security guard actually came over and told him to turn it down. Gary was eating his powdered donuts, of course, and he got a smudge of white powder right on Dave's new screen when he pointed at it. Dave was furious. He spent like ten minutes trying to wipe it off with his neon vest, but those vests are made of that cheap scratchy material, so it just smeared the donut grease around. \n\nBut yeah, even if Dave took a video of me from every angle and swore up and down that I looked normal, I wouldn't believe it. The camera doesn't fix my face, you know? It's just... it's crooked. I can feel it. I know it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you believe your face is not crooked if your family member took a video of you from multiple angles and it showed that your face looks normal?\n\nPatient: A family member? Like my sister Sarah? I mean... no, honestly. Look, I know I'm repeating myself here, but like I said earlier, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this chair is because I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's constantly in my head. I feel like every single person at the warehouse is staring at it, or the people at salsa night, even when they swear to me they don't see anything wrong. So Sarah showing me a video isn't going to magically make me believe them. \n\nShe actually did have her camera out at my nephew's birthday party a couple weeks ago. He just turned seven. They rented one of those giant inflatable obstacle courses for the backyard, but the guy who delivered it forgot the tarp that goes underneath to protect the grass. So my brother-in-law Mark had to drive over to the hardware store on a Saturday morning to buy one. You ever try going to the hardware store on a weekend? It's absolute chaos. People just leave those heavy-duty flatbed carts right in the middle of the aisles, and the contractors act like they own the place. Mark was gone for like two hours, and by the time he got back, the kids had already gotten into the coolers and drank all the sodas. They were just running around the yard completely wired on sugar, chasing the dog. \n\nAnd Mark ended up buying this cheap, thin plastic tarp that ripped the second they dragged the inflatable over it anyway. He was sweating through his shirt trying to tape it together with duct tape, getting so mad at the delivery guy who was just sitting in his truck smoking a cigarette. \n\nSo Sarah is standing there filming this whole disaster, and she points her phone at me while I'm holding a paper plate with a hot dog on it. I just turned around and walked into the house. I don't care if she's my sister, I know what I look like. A video from her is just going to show exactly what I already see in the mirror.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I feel like we're going in circles a bit here, doc. Like I've been saying, a medical report wouldn't change anything at all. The whole reason I'm here is because I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's just playing on a loop in my head all day. I go to work at the warehouse, or out to salsa night, and I just know everyone is noticing it and staring at me, even when they swear to me they don't see anything wrong. So no, a piece of paper from a doctor isn't going to fix what I'm looking at in the mirror. 

Speaking of the warehouse, though, it's been an absolute nightmare there this week. They hired this new forklift driver because Jimmy finally retired. Jimmy was a legend, honestly, he could thread a double-wide pallet through the tightest aisles without even looking. But this new guy—Greg, I think his name is—he completely backed into the main shelving unit in aisle four on Tuesday. Just smashed right into it. He knocked over maybe fifty boxes of industrial paper towels and a bunch of heavy-duty degreaser. 

It took us like three hours to clean up the mess, and our manager, Mr. Henderson, just stood there the whole time holding his clipboard, not lifting a single finger to help us. He always wears these cheap clip-on ties because he thinks it makes him look like some big shot executive, but one time last month it literally fell right off his collar into a massive bin of packing peanuts. We were all trying so hard not to laugh while he was digging around for it. 

But yeah, anyway... no, a medical report wouldn't change my mind. My face is just messed up, and I know it is.

He spent the rest of the break playing some racing game on his phone with the volume turned all the way up. The sound effects were so loud, Gary the security guard actually came over and told him to turn it down. Gary was eating his powdered donuts, of course, and he got a smudge of white powder right on Dave's new screen when he pointed at it. Dave was furious. He spent like ten minutes trying to wipe it off with his neon vest, but those vests are made of that cheap scratchy material, so it just smeared the donut grease around. \n\nBut yeah, even if Dave took a video of me from every angle and swore up and down that I looked normal, I wouldn't believe it. The camera doesn't fix my face, you know? It's just... it's crooked. I can feel it. I know it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you believe your face is not crooked if your family member took a video of you from multiple angles and it showed that your face looks normal?\n\nPatient: A family member? Like my sister Sarah? I mean... no, honestly. Look, I know I'm repeating myself here, but like I said earlier, the whole reason I'm even sitting in this chair is because I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's constantly in my head. I feel like every single person at the warehouse is staring at it, or the people at salsa night, even when they swear to me they don't see anything wrong. So Sarah showing me a video isn't going to magically make me believe them. \n\nShe actually did have her camera out at my nephew's birthday party a couple weeks ago. He just turned seven. They rented one of those giant inflatable obstacle courses for the backyard, but the guy who delivered it forgot the tarp that goes underneath to protect the grass. So my brother-in-law Mark had to drive over to the hardware store on a Saturday morning to buy one. You ever try going to the hardware store on a weekend? It's absolute chaos. People just leave those heavy-duty flatbed carts right in the middle of the aisles, and the contractors act like they own the place. Mark was gone for like two hours, and by the time he got back, the kids had already gotten into the coolers and drank all the sodas. They were just running around the yard completely wired on sugar, chasing the dog. \n\nAnd Mark ended up buying this cheap, thin plastic tarp that ripped the second they dragged the inflatable over it anyway. He was sweating through his shirt trying to tape it together with duct tape, getting so mad at the delivery guy who was just sitting in his truck smoking a cigarette. \n\nSo Sarah is standing there filming this whole disaster, and she points her phone at me while I'm holding a paper plate with a hot dog on it. I just turned around and walked into the house. I don't care if she's my sister, I know what I look like. A video from her is just going to show exactly what I already see in the mirror.\n\nDoctor: Would your belief about your face being crooked change if you were shown a medical report confirming that there's nothing physically wrong with it, but you still see it as crooked?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles a bit here, doc. Like I've been saying, a medical report wouldn't change anything at all. The whole reason I'm here is because I just can't stop thinking about how the left side of my face looks completely crooked and messed up. It's just playing on a loop in my head all day. I go to work at the warehouse, or out to salsa night, and I just know everyone is noticing it and staring at me, even when they swear to me they don't see anything wrong. So no, a piece of paper from a doctor isn't going to fix what I'm looking at in the mirror. \n\nSpeaking of the warehouse, though, it's been an absolute nightmare there this week. They hired this new forklift driver because Jimmy finally retired. Jimmy was a legend, honestly, he could thread a double-wide pallet through the tightest aisles without even looking. But this new guy—Greg, I think his name is—he completely backed into the main shelving unit in aisle four on Tuesday. Just smashed right into it. He knocked over maybe fifty boxes of industrial paper towels and a bunch of heavy-duty degreaser. \n\nIt took us like three hours to clean up the mess, and our manager, Mr. Henderson, just stood there the whole time holding his clipboard, not lifting a single finger to help us. He always wears these cheap clip-on ties because he thinks it makes him look like some big shot executive, but one time last month it literally fell right off his collar into a massive bin of packing peanuts. We were all trying so hard not to laugh while he was digging around for it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... no, a medical report wouldn't change my mind. My face is just messed up, and I know it is."
  }
